- **Pricing**: Tag "Complimentary Tea" — Welcoming mint tea and gallery viewing are completely free; original canvas paintings and sculptures carry clear artist-direct prices. (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "Original Art" — Genuine cultural space exhibiting original contemporary paintings, calligraphy, and handcrafted desert wood sculptures made on-site. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Effortless" — Level walk inside the village with seated indoor salon viewing; completely accessible to travelers of all physical conditions. (good)

### 🌿 Wellness & Living Arts (2)
### 20. Traditional Berber Cooking Class (id: merzouga-cooking-class)

- **Safety**: Tag "Food Safe" — Clean, home-style kitchen environments with well-regulated cooking stations and fresh, thoroughly cooked seasonal ingredients. (good)
- **Scams**: Tag "Transparent Terms" — Bookings via established riads or family homestays specify all inclusions up front, from souk shopping to dining on the final meal. (good)
- **Pricing**: Tag "Fixed Price" — Transparently priced at 250–400 MAD (~€25–€40) per person, covering market ingredients, mint tea, chef guidance, and the prepared feast. (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "Homestyle Traditional" — Genuine hands-on instruction from local Amazigh home cooks, preparing regional tagines, bread, or madfouna with family spice blends. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Beginner Friendly" — Light activity involving vegetable chopping, spice blending, and dough kneading; seated prep is readily accommodated for all ages. (good)

---

### 21. Relaxing Hammam & Spa Session (id: merzouga-hammam)

- **Safety**: Tag "Slick Floors" — Wet marble and tiled floors are slippery underfoot; attendants monitor steam levels and supply clean exfoliating gloves (*kessa*). (caution)
- **Scams**: Tag "Published Menus" — Reputable hotel and riad spas provide clear printed treatment menus specifying session lengths and included body products. (good)
- **Pricing**: Tag "Tiered Pricing" — Local public village baths cost 20–50 MAD (excluding products/tips), while private riad spa rituals with scrubs and argan massages run 300–600 MAD (~€30–€60). (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "Traditional Ritual" — Authentic centuries-old Moroccan thermal cleansing using eucalyptus black olive soap (*savon noir*), vigorous exfoliation, and rhassoul clay. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Heat Sensitive" — Completely passive and restful, though intense steam room heat may cause lightheadedness for travelers sensitive to high temperatures. (caution)
## Ouarzazate — 20 listings
> Files: `src/listings/things/ouarzazate/` — 8 heritage · 12 nature

### 🏛️ Heritage & Culture (8)
### 1. Aït Ben Haddou (id: ouarzazate-a-t-ben-haddou)

- **Safety**: Tag "Uneven Steps" — Traversing ancient earthen alleys involves weathered clay stairs, loose stones, and unrailed drop-offs; use the modern pedestrian bridge to cross the river safely. (caution)
- **Scams**: Tag "Unofficial Touts" — Entrance to the public UNESCO ksar is free; disregard unauthorized touts demanding entry tickets or aggressive tolls at private house doorways. (caution)
- **Pricing**: Tag "Free Entry" — Public site access is completely free of charge; a few privately restored kasbah houses inside charge a modest 10–20 MAD fee for roof access. (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "UNESCO Landmark" — Genuine 17th-century earthen clay *ksar* showcasing traditional pre-Saharan architecture, though most families live in the modern village across the river. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Steep Incline" — Reaching the summit granary (*agadir*) requires a 20–30 minute uphill hike on gravel trails and steep, irregular dirt steps under direct sun. (caution)

---

### 2. Atlas Studios (id: ouarzazate-atlas-studios)

- **Safety**: Tag "Sun Exposed" — Expansive open-air desert grounds with little shade and midday heat; watch your footing around fragile plaster and weathered faux-stone facades. (caution)
- **Scams**: Tag "Guided Inclusions" — Standard admission includes an official guided tour of the backlots; clarify guide gratuities and extra fees for off-site sets beforehand. (good)
- **Pricing**: Tag "Fixed Tickets" — Official entry is 80–130 MAD (~€8–€13) per person, with an optional separate ticket (~40 MAD) required for the remote *Kingdom of Heaven* desert set. (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "Movie Illusions" — Authentic international film facility operating since 1983, displaying real full-scale film sets and props crafted from wood, plaster, and foam. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Moderate Walk" — Guided tours involve 60–90 minutes of steady walking across mostly flat, unpaved dirt lots and sandy backlot pathways. (good)

---

### 3. Taourirt Kasbah (id: ouarzazate-taourirt-kasbah)

- **Safety**: Tag "Low Ceilings" — The interior contains low doorways, dim corridors, and steep, winding earthen stairways that require careful footing and head clearance. (caution)
- **Scams**: Tag "Persistent Touts" — Unofficial street guides congregate outside claiming the kasbah is closed to steer tourists into craft shops; buy tickets only at the official counter. (caution)
- **Pricing**: Tag "Official Fee" — Admission to the restored municipal palace sections is fixed at 20–30 MAD (~€2–€3); licensed local guides can be hired optionally for 50–100 MAD. (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "Glaoui Fortress" — Authentic 19th-century fortress of Marrakech Pasha Thami El Glaoui, featuring intact cedar ceilings, stucco work, and geometric mudbrick towers. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Narrow Stairs" — Exploring the multi-level complex requires climbing several flights of narrow, irregular clay stairs without elevators or ramps. (caution)

---

### 4. Cinema Museum (id: ouarzazate-cinema-museum)

- **Safety**: Tag "Well Maintained" — Enclosed galleries and paved courtyards located right on Avenue Mohammed V; safe, level, and hazard-free for all ages. (good)
- **Scams**: Tag "Direct Entry" — Transparently run with an official ticket office directly across from Taourirt Kasbah, free from tour redirection traps or hard sales pitches. (good)
- **Pricing**: Tag "Fixed Ticket" — Clear and posted admission fee of 80 MAD (~€8) for adults and 40 MAD for children, paid directly at the entrance window. (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "Reused Props" — Housed in a genuine 1981 Italian film studio, presenting authentic vintage cameras, stage lighting rigs, costumes, and retired movie sets. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Effortless" — Flat walking surface across single-level exhibition halls and open courtyards, easily explored within 45 to 60 minutes. (good)

---

### 5. CLA Studios Ouarzazate (id: ouarzazate-cla-studios-ouarzazate)

- **Safety**: Tag "Sun Exposed" — Extensive outdoor sets exposed to desert heat; watch your step around aging wooden platforms, stage scaffolds, and gravel surfaces. (caution)
- **Scams**: Tag "Production Closure" — Tour access periodically closes without warning when active film crews occupy the backlots; check operating status prior to traveling. (caution)
- **Pricing**: Tag "Fixed Admission" — When open for public tours, entry is clearly set at approximately 80–100 MAD (~€8–€10) per adult for sound stages and outdoor sets. (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "Film Backdrops" — Working studio founded in 2004 alongside Dino De Laurentiis, housing genuine outdoor movie sets built for major historical productions. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Flat Walking" — Low physical exertion along flat dirt and gravel tracks between outdoor sets, lasting around 45–60 minutes. (good)

---

### 6. Old Synagogue & Mellah Walk (id: ouarzazate-old-synagogue-and-mellah-walk)

- **Safety**: Tag "Uneven Alleys" — Safe pedestrian quarter during daylight hours, though unpaved earthen alleys have uneven dirt surfaces and dim covered corridors. (good)
- **Scams**: Tag "Shop Diversion" — Self-appointed guides offer to lead you to the "old synagogue," which now operates primarily as a commercial carpet and antique showroom. (caution)
- **Pricing**: Tag "Free Entry" — Wandering the historic Jewish quarter is free of charge; visiting the prayer room is free, though a small courtesy gratuity or purchase is customary. (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "Shared History" — Historic mudbrick *Mellah* community built beside Taourirt Kasbah, preserving Hebrew inscriptions and Judeo-Amazigh artisan heritage. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Gentle Stroll" — Leisurely walk on level ground through quiet residential alleys with only occasional low steps and entry thresholds. (good)

---

### 7. Museum-Theatre Memory of Ouarzazate (id: ouarzazate-museum-theatre-memory-of-ouarzazate)

- **Safety**: Tag "Very Safe" — Intimate and sheltered ethnographic exhibition space with clear walking pathways and quiet indoor rooms. (good)
- **Scams**: Tag "Warm Welcome" — Small community-run private cultural center with hospitable reception and no high-pressure commercial pitches or fake guide tactics. (good)
- **Pricing**: Tag "Modest Fee" — Admission is inexpensive, typically 20–30 MAD (~€2–€3) per person or voluntary contribution, directly maintaining the exhibits. (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "Local Ethnography" — Authentic presentation of southern Moroccan Berber daily life, displaying preserved agricultural tools, garments, and traditional crafts. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Effortless" — Compact single-level floor plan requiring minimal walking, suitable for visitors of all physical capabilities and age groups. (good)

---

### 8. Taourirt Kasbah Rooftop Tea Stop (id: ouarzazate-taourirt-kasbah-rooftop-tea-stop)

- **Safety**: Tag "Low Railings" — Traditional cafe setting, but upper terrace perimeter walls and railings can be low; supervise young children near rooftop ledges. (caution)
- **Scams**: Tag "Clear Menus" — Cafes around the kasbah square and ksar terraces offer standard hospitality; review printed drink menus beforehand to avoid tourist markups. (good)
- **Pricing**: Tag "Modest Cost" — Standard fresh Moroccan mint tea ranges from 15–25 MAD (~€1.50–€2.50) per pot; pastries and cold beverages are priced nominally. (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "Classic Pause" — Traditional Moroccan tea break served with fresh mint and sugar, accompanied by panoramic views of the mudbrick towers and Atlas peaks. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Stair Climb" — Accessing the rooftop vantage points requires climbing one to two flights of standard or narrow outdoor stairs. (caution)

### 🏜️ Nature & Adventure (12)
### 9. Fint Oasis (id: ouarzazate-fint-oasis)

- **Safety**: Tag "Rough Piste" — Reached via an unpaved, stony desert track prone to washouts after rain; rocky riverbanks and wet crossing stones require steady footing. (caution)
- **Scams**: Tag "Self Appointed" — Village youths frequently offer unsolicited guided walks or invitations to tea expecting tips; decline politely if exploring independently. (caution)
- **Pricing**: Tag "Free Entry" — Wandering the palm grove and river valley is free; refreshing mint tea at village auberges costs 15–25 MAD, and voluntary guide tips run 30–50 MAD. (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "Working Oasis" — Genuine Amazigh agricultural valley where four quiet mudbrick hamlets cultivate date palms, almonds, and vegetables along a natural riverbed. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Moderate Walk" — Mostly level walking along dirt farming trails and dry river sand, with occasional stepping-stone stream crossings and optional rocky ridge climbs. (good)

---

### 10. Kasbah of Tifoultoute (id: ouarzazate-kasbah-of-tifoultoute)

- **Safety**: Tag "Weathered Adobe" — Ancient earthen fortress with unrestored wings, cracked mud floors, and panoramic rooftop terraces featuring low parapets without guardrails. (caution)
- **Scams**: Tag "Informal Access" — Maintained semi-privately by local caretakers; verify what your payment covers (interior access vs. rooftop tea service) before handing over cash. (caution)
- **Pricing**: Tag "Nominal Fee" — Entrance is inexpensive at 10–20 MAD (~€1–€2) per person, with an optional 15–25 MAD charge for mint tea on the panoramic viewing terrace. (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "Atmospheric Ruin" — Historic 17th-to-19th-century Glaoui stronghold featured in *Lawrence of Arabia*, preserved in its authentic weathered, semi-decayed state. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Steep Climb" — Requires a brief uphill walk to the hilltop gate followed by navigating narrow, dark, and uneven clay stairways to reach the roof. (caution)

---

### 11. Kasbah Amridil (id: ouarzazate-kasbah-amridil)

- **Safety**: Tag "Low Doorways" — Well-restored and structurally solid, but retains historic low lintels, dim passages, steep clay stairs, and open rooftop ramparts. (caution)
- **Scams**: Tag "Dual Entrances" — The kasbah is divided into two separate family wings with competing ticket counters; verify which wing or museum exhibit you wish to tour. (caution)
- **Pricing**: Tag "Fixed Ticket" — Clear and posted admission fee of 20–30 MAD (~€2–€3) per person; optional tours with knowledgeable on-site family guides cost around 40–50 MAD. (good)
- **Authenticity**: Tag "Living Museum" — Remarkable 17th-century fortified mudbrick complex featured on the historic 50 MAD banknote, showcasing genuine traditional olive presses, bread ovens, and tools. (good)
- **Difficulty**: Tag "Narrow Steps" — Multi-level layout requires ascending several flights of narrow, winding earthen steps to reach the watchtowers and roof battlements. (caution)

## Rabat — 20 listings
> Files: `src/listings/things/rabat/` — 13 heritage · 6 nature · 1 wellness

### 🏛️ Heritage & Culture (13)
### 1. Kasbah des Oudaias (id: rabat-kasbah-des-oudaias)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Patrolled" — Regular tourist police patrols monitor the gates and streets; paths require care along cliff viewpoints. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Rabat / Her Safe Voyage)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Persistent Touts" — Unofficial faux guides linger near Bab Oudaia and pushy henna women wait by the garden gates. *(Tone: caution | Source: Serenity Morocco Tours)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Entry" — Wandering the fortress streets, Andalusian Gardens, and coastal platform is free; only the National Jewelry Museum charges entry. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Rabat)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Living Citadel" — A genuine 12th-century Almohad fortress still inhabited by local families, preserving authentic Andalusian architecture. *(Tone: good | Source: UNESCO / Visit Rabat)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Uneven Steps" — Cobbled slopes, narrow alleyways, and stone stairs leading to viewing platforms restrict stroller and wheelchair access. *(Tone: caution | Source: MaJourneys)*

---

### 2. Hassan Tower (id: rabat-hassan-tower)

- **Safety:** Tag "Royal-Guarded" — Gated complex under 24/7 protection by the Moroccan Royal Guard and state security forces. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Rabat)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Scam-Free" — Touts and fake guides are strictly forbidden from entering the historical esplanade. *(Tone: good | Source: Tour Guide Rabat)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Entry" — Completely open to the public daily without entry fees, ticket bookings, or hidden charges. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Rabat / Memphis Tours)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Almohad Original" — An untouched 12th-century Almohad sandstone minaret and unfinished mosque column field from Sultan Yacoub al-Mansour. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Rabat)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Level Ground" — Wide, flat paved esplanade with step-free surfaces, though expansive and without shade in midday heat. *(Tone: good | Source: Tour Guide Rabat)*

---

### 3. Mausoleum of Mohammed V (id: rabat-mausoleum-of-mohammed-v)

- **Safety:** Tag "Heavily Secured" — Continuously monitored by ceremonial Royal Guards on horseback and foot within an official state sanctuary. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Rabat / STORY Rabat)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Strictly Regulated" — Touts and unlicensed guides are barred inside; guards strictly enforce respectful conduct and conservative attire. *(Tone: good | Source: FindTourGuide)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Entry" — Complimentary admission for all visitors; no entry tickets, audio-guide fees, or required donations. *(Tone: good | Source: Hertz Morocco / Tour Guide Rabat)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Handcrafted Masterpiece" — Active royal pantheon hand-built by over 400 Moroccan artisans with authentic carved cedar, marble, and zellige tilework. *(Tone: good | Source: Barceló Experiences / Visit Rabat)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Ramp-Accessible" — Smooth stone courtyard with gentle ramps leading directly up to the interior gallery viewing balcony. *(Tone: good | Source: STORY Rabat)*

### 🏜️ Nature & Adventure (6)
### 14. Andalusian Gardens (id: rabat-andalusian-gardens)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Gated" — Enclosed within fortress ramparts with municipal attendants on duty; peaceful, well-maintained, and safe for solo visitors. *(Tone: good | Source: Her Safe Voyage)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Persistent Touts" — Unofficial guides and henna vendors often linger near the entry gates; decline unsolicited offers and explore independently. *(Tone: caution | Source: Her Safe Voyage)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Entry" — Public access to the gardens and shaded courtyards is completely free; only the adjacent Oudayas Museum requires an entry fee. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Morocco Tours)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Moorish Heritage" — Authentic 20th-century botanical sanctuary designed in classical Andalusian style with historical water channels, citrus trees, and bougainvillea. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Morocco Tours / UNESCO)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Mostly Flat" — Level, smooth gravel walkways with ample shaded benches throughout, though a few stone stairways connect to the upper kasbah exits. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Morocco Tours)*

---

### 15. Rabat Beach (id: rabat-rabat-beach)

- **Safety:** Tag "Strong Currents" — Atlantic rip currents and waves can be hazardous; Protection Civile lifeguards patrol during peak summer, but warning flags must be respected. *(Tone: caution | Source: STORY Le Carrousel / Morocco 2030 Hotels)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Unregulated Rentals" — Chair, umbrella, and surfboard vendors rarely display price lists; negotiate and lock in the rate before accepting equipment. *(Tone: caution | Source: Agoda / Wanderlog)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Access" — Open municipal beach with free public entry; gear rentals and local surf school lessons carry modest local rates (~50–150 MAD). *(Tone: good | Source: Morocco 2030 Hotels)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Local Gathering" — An authentic, uncommercialized city beach where Rabat residents, neighborhood football games, and homegrown surf clubs gather daily. *(Tone: good | Source: Wanderlog / Serenity Morocco Tours)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Demanding Surf" — Strolling the paved promenade is effortless, but trudging through deep dry sand and swimming against open Atlantic swells require physical stamina. *(Tone: caution | Source: STORY Le Carrousel)*

---

### 16. Bouregreg Marina Walk (id: rabat-bouregreg-marina-walk)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Secured" — Modern riverside promenade with dedicated security personnel, CCTV coverage, ample night lighting, and quay safety equipment. *(Tone: good | Source: Haqqi Tours / Top Sightseeing)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Low Risk" — Minimal touting along the docks; when taking a traditional wooden *flouka* boat across to Salé, confirm the standard fare (~2.5–5 MAD) upfront. *(Tone: good | Source: Morocco Amazing Destination / Plantrip)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Transparent Pricing" — The promenade is entirely free to walk; marina restaurants, cafés, and boat tours display fixed, printed menus. *(Tone: good | Source: Serenity Morocco Tours / Top Sightseeing)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Living Estuary" — A modern waterfront promenade coexisting with traditional wooden rowboats that have ferried commuters between Rabat and Salé for centuries. *(Tone: good | Source: Serenity Morocco Tours / Plantrip)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Fully Accessible" — Broad, flat, smoothly paved pedestrian walkway with zero elevation change or steps, completely accessible for strollers and wheelchairs. *(Tone: good | Source: Top Sightseeing / Haqqi Tours)*

### 🌿 Wellness & Living Arts (1)
### 20. Les Jardins Exotiques de Bouknadel (id: rabat-les-jardins-exotiques-de-bouknadel)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Maintained" — Enclosed botanical reserve featuring marked paths, staff supervision, and railings along wooden footbridges and aquatic ponds. *(Tone: good | Source: Fondation FM6E / Activityz Maroc)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Foundation-Run" — Formally managed by the Mohammed VI Foundation for Environmental Protection with official gate ticketing; no touts or unlicensed guides on the grounds. *(Tone: good | Source: Fondation FM6E)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Fixed Price" — Standard published entry of 20 MAD for adults and 12 MAD for children, granting full access to the gardens, reptile vivarium, and founder's museum. *(Tone: good | Source: Jardins Exotiques Official)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Historic Arboretum" — Authentic 4.5-hectare national heritage sanctuary laid out in 1952 by horticulturalist Marcel François, preserving over 600 plant species across 14 global biomes. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Rabat / Ecologie.ma)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Partially Accessible" — Mostly level, shaded walking along two marked loop trails, though narrow side passages, wooden bridges, and steps limit wheelchairs and strollers. *(Tone: caution | Source: Hertz Morocco / MaJourneys)*

## Saidia — 20 listings
> Files: `src/listings/things/saidia/` — 5 heritage · 15 nature

### 🏛️ Heritage & Culture (5)
### 1. Souk de Saïdia (id: saidia-souk-de-sa-dia)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Secured" — Situated in a calm, compact town center with regular police patrols; safe for evening walks, though summer crowds warrant standard pocket awareness. *(Tone: good | Source: Radisson Travel / Sail The World)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Low-Pressure" — Vendors are far less aggressive than in major tourist hubs; no pushy guide syndicates, though basic price negotiation is customary for non-food items. *(Tone: good | Source: Memphis Tours / Moroccan Mate)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Bargain-Friendly" — Free entry; everyday snacks and household goods have low local prices, while handicrafts and souvenirs require friendly bargaining. *(Tone: good | Source: Ona Hotels / Moroccan Mate)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Unstaged Local" — A functioning commercial hub serving coastal residents and domestic summer vacationers rather than a staged spectacle. *(Tone: good | Source: Radisson Travel / Memphis Tours)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Flat Paved" — Located entirely on flat terrain with level streets and shopfronts; easily walkable for seniors and fully accessible for strollers. *(Tone: good | Source: SeniorTravelWithUs)*

---

### 2. Sunday Open-Air Souk (id: saidia-sunday-open-air-souk)

- **Safety:** Tag "Densely Packed" — High concentration of shoppers and vehicles during peak morning hours; keep cash secure and watch footing near temporary vendor tarp lines. *(Tone: caution | Source: Nature & Patrimoine / YouTube Mor Acro)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Scam-Free" — An authentic rural farmers' gathering with zero tourist touts; produce is weighed openly on commercial scales in plain view. *(Tone: good | Source: Memphis Tours / Moroccan Mate)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Cash-Only" — No entrance fees; agricultural produce, spices, and household goods are sold at cheap regional rates payable strictly in small Moroccan Dirham cash. *(Tone: good | Source: Moroccan Mate)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Deeply Traditional" — Authentic weekly open-air souk where Triffa plain and Beni Snassen mountain farmers trade fresh harvests directly with local families. *(Tone: good | Source: Memphis Tours / YouTube Mor Acro)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Rough Dirt" — Unpaved dirt ground, dust, and narrow passageways between makeshift vendor canopies make wheelchair and stroller navigation difficult. *(Tone: caution | Source: Moroccan Mate)*

---

### 3. Marché Couvert (id: saidia-march-couvert)

- **Safety:** Tag "Slippery Aisles" — Municipal indoor facility with clear exits, but concrete and tiled pathways near fish and poultry counters can be wet and slick. *(Tone: caution | Source: Trip.com / Moroccan Mate)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Scale-Regulated" — Licensed municipal vendors operating from permanent stalls; simply ensure scales are zeroed and confirm the kilo rate before fish is scaled. *(Tone: good | Source: Moroccan Mate)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Market Rates" — Free public access; fresh Mediterranean catches, meat, and seasonal produce are priced openly by the kilogram at local market value. *(Tone: good | Source: Moroccan Mate)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Daily Working" — Completely genuine municipal food hall where Saïdia home cooks and neighborhood chefs purchase daily provisions. *(Tone: good | Source: Memphis Tours / Moroccan Mate)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Step-Free" — Single-level covered facility with wide entrances and flat tiled aisles, allowing straightforward access for strollers and mobility aids. *(Tone: good | Source: Moroccan Mate)*

---

### 4. Saïdia Kasbah (id: saidia-sa-dia-kasbah)

- **Safety:** Tag "Quietly Patrolled" — Located in a quiet central town district close to civil administration offices and residential streets with dependable daytime security. *(Tone: good | Source: CRI Oriental / Radisson Hotels)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Self-Guided" — No official entrance fees or gate attendants; disregard any unofficial individuals requesting payment to view or photograph the fortress walls. *(Tone: good | Source: Wanderlog / Lume Travel)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Completely Free" — Walking around the historic 1883 fortress perimeter and adjacent public plazas costs nothing and requires no tickets. *(Tone: good | Source: Moroccan Mate / Wanderlog)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Alawite Heritage" — An authentic 19th-century frontier fortification constructed in 1883 by Sultan Hassan I to defend Morocco's eastern border. *(Tone: good | Source: CRI Oriental / Le360)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Effortlessly Flat" — Positioned on level coastal ground with smooth surrounding sidewalks, enabling effortless strolls for visitors of all mobility levels. *(Tone: good | Source: SeniorTravelWithUs)*

---

### 5. Berkane Orange Market Day Trip (id: saidia-berkane-orange-market-day-trip)

- **Safety:** Tag "Highway-Linked" — Smooth, well-paved 25 km multi-lane regional road (R602) connects Saïdia to Berkane with licensed grand taxis and regular intercity traffic. *(Tone: good | Source: Wikivoyage / Sahara Discovery)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Regulated Taxis" — Official grand taxis run regular fixed-route lines between town stations; clarify whether you are buying single seats or chartering the whole vehicle. *(Tone: good | Source: Sahara Discovery / Wikivoyage)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Budget-Friendly" — Standard grand taxi transit costs roughly 15–20 MAD per seat; seasonal citrus at roadside orchards and local markets costs only a few Dirhams per kilo. *(Tone: good | Source: Sahara Discovery Tours)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Citrus Heartland" — Fully authentic excursion to the agricultural capital of eastern Morocco, renowned for extensive clementine orchards and working produce souks. *(Tone: good | Source: Unveiling Morocco / Wikivoyage)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Gentle Walking" — Involves standard town strolling and market browsing on flat urban streets, though navigating busy road crossings around market zones requires care. *(Tone: good | Source: Wikivoyage / Moroccan Mate)*

### 🏜️ Nature & Adventure (15)
### 6. Plage de Saïdia (id: saidia-plage-de-sa-dia)

- **Safety:** Tag "Seasonally Lifeguarded" — Protection Civile lifeguards patrol and fly flag alerts during summer months; waters are shallow and calm, though designated motorized watersport corridors should be avoided. *(Tone: good | Source: Protection Civile Maroc / Visit Saidia)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Unregulated Rentals" — Beachfront umbrella and lounger operators lack posted price tags; always confirm the daily rental price before accepting a sunbed. *(Tone: caution | Source: MorTraveling / Lume Travel)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Access" — Public access to the entire 14 km Mediterranean coastline is completely free; optional umbrella and sun lounger sets rent for ~30–70 MAD per day. *(Tone: good | Source: MorTraveling / Ona Hotels)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Authentic Holiday" — A classic Moroccan summer holiday destination where domestic families and returning diaspora gather for sunset seaside leisure, mint tea, and beach football. *(Tone: good | Source: MorTraveling)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Gentle Slope" — Very wide, firm sand flats with shallow, gradual water entry and ramped corniche access points suitable for strollers and seniors. *(Tone: good | Source: Radisson Travel / MorTraveling)*

---

### 7. Marina Saïdia Promenade (id: saidia-marina-sa-dia-promenade)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Guarded" — Private resort development featuring 24/7 dedicated security patrols, CCTV surveillance, night lighting, and lifebuoy stations along all quays. *(Tone: good | Source: Agoda / Radisson Blu)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Licensed Providers" — Regulated commercial marina where yacht charters, dive shops, and boat cruise desks operate under official maritime permits. *(Tone: good | Source: Marina Saidia Official / MorTraveling)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Fixed Price" — The boardwalk is free to explore; marina cafés, ice cream shops, and waterfront restaurants post itemized, printed menus. *(Tone: good | Source: MorTraveling / Saidia Resort)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Modern Resort" — Purpose-built international yachting port and commercial leisure strip rather than a historic Moroccan cultural quarter. *(Tone: caution | Source: MorTraveling)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Fully Accessible" — Broad, flat, smoothly paved pedestrian quays with zero gradient or stairs, making it effortlessly accessible for wheelchairs and strollers. *(Tone: good | Source: MorTraveling / Agoda)*

---

### 8. Med-Saïdia Corniche Bike Ride (id: saidia-med-sa-dia-corniche-bike-ride)

- **Safety:** Tag "Paved Promenade" — Wide, car-free coastal boulevard (Boulevard Mohamed V) with designated cycling areas and evening lighting, though summer pedestrian traffic requires moderate speed. *(Tone: good | Source: MorTraveling)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Independent Rentals" — Informal bike and quadricycle (rosalie) rental stands line the corniche; check brakes and gears, and agree on rental duration and deposit terms before departing. *(Tone: caution | Source: MorTraveling)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Hourly Rates" — Riding your own bicycle is free; standard bicycle rentals cost ~30–50 MAD per hour, while multi-passenger family pedal carts cost ~100–150 MAD per hour. *(Tone: good | Source: MorTraveling)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Family Tradition" — A genuine summer tradition where Moroccan holidaymakers rent pedal carts and cruise the seaside corniche at twilight. *(Tone: good | Source: MorTraveling / Radisson Blu)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Effortlessly Flat" — Completely level asphalt and tiled promenade stretching for miles with no inclines, suitable for riders of any fitness level. *(Tone: good | Source: MorTraveling)*

---

### 9. Moulouya Estuary Birdwatching (id: saidia-moulouya-estuary-birdwatching)

- **Safety:** Tag "Unsupervised Wilds" — Protected wild wetland with no lifeguards or fixed ranger stations; stay on firm footpaths and avoid river mouth currents and deep marsh mud. *(Tone: caution | Source: IUCN / Ramsar RSIS)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Ungated Site" — Open public nature reserve with no official entrance fee; disregard anyone demanding gate payments, and book any guided boat trips through established eco-guides. *(Tone: good | Source: GREPOM / Ramsar.org)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Completely Free" — Free entry for birdwatchers and nature lovers walking the coastal dunes, salt marshes, and lagoon trails. *(Tone: good | Source: Ramsar.org / IUCN)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Protected Wetland" — Nationally classified SIBE and international Ramsar wetland hosting over 200 wild migratory bird species, including flamingos and Audouin's gulls, in an untouched habitat. *(Tone: good | Source: Ramsar.org / GREPOM)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Sandy Trekking" — Walking entails unpaved sandy tracks, coastal dunes, and muddy wetland shores; requires sturdy footwear and is not accessible for wheelchairs or strollers. *(Tone: caution | Source: IUCN / Ramsar RSIS)*

## Tangier — 20 listings
> Files: `src/listings/things/tangier/` — 12 heritage · 7 nature · 1 wellness

### 🏛️ Heritage & Culture (12)
### 1. Kasbah Museum (Dar el-Makhzen) (id: tangier-kasbah-museum)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Guarded" — Fortified historic citadel complex monitored by museum security staff and regular tourist police patrols. *(Tone: good | Source: FNM / Barceló Experiences)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Official Counter" — Managed directly by the Fondation Nationale des Musées with a formal ticket booth; decline unofficial guides waiting at Place de la Kasbah. *(Tone: good | Source: Departly / Morocco Amazing Destination)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Fixed Admission" — Transparent state-regulated ticket pricing (~20–30 MAD standard, up to 50 MAD for combo tickets) with zero surprise fees. *(Tone: good | Source: FNM / TangerLocal)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Sultan Palace" — Genuine 18th-century palace of Ahmed Ben Ali and former sultans, housing original Roman mosaics from Volubilis and ancient artifacts. *(Tone: good | Source: FNM / UNESCO)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Steep Walk" — Reaching the museum requires an uphill trek across uneven medina cobblestones and climbing internal palace steps. *(Tone: caution | Source: Departly / Barceló)*

---

### 2. Tangier American Legation Museum (id: tangier-legation-museum)

- **Safety:** Tag "Secure Landmark" — Well-maintained diplomatic property and research center operated by TALIM with attentive staff and security protocols. *(Tone: good | Source: TALIM / Barceló)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Regulated NGO" — Accredited cultural center and U.S. National Historic Landmark; entry tickets and museum shop items are processed strictly at the reception desk. *(Tone: good | Source: TALIM Official)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Fixed Entry" — Clear published admission fee (50 MAD for non-residents, 20 MAD for residents) clearly stated at entry. *(Tone: good | Source: TALIM Official / TangerLocal)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Historic Diplomacy" — Tangier's oldest American diplomatic asset abroad (gifted in 1821 by Sultan Moulay Suliman), featuring genuine historic furnishings and Paul Bowles archives. *(Tone: good | Source: TALIM)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Multi-Level Stairs" — Multi-story historic townhouse connected by narrow staircases and corridors with no elevator access. *(Tone: caution | Source: Blondie in Morocco)*

---

### 3. Grand Socco (Place du 9 Avril 1947) (id: tangier-grand-socco)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Monitored" — Constant pedestrian activity and visible tourist police presence, though road traffic circling the central fountain demands attention. *(Tone: good | Source: TangerLocal / Lonely Planet)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Persistent Touts" — Faux guides and street hawkers frequently approach travelers near Bab el-Fahs; politely decline unrequested assistance. *(Tone: caution | Source: Lonely Planet / Departly)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Access" — A public civic square open 24/7 without admission charges; surrounding cafés offer standard, cheap menu rates. *(Tone: good | Source: TangerLocal)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Civic Crossroads" — Historic seam where the medina meets the Ville Nouvelle, and the symbolic site of Sultan Mohammed V's 1947 Moroccan independence speech. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Morocco / Lonely Planet)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Gently Sloping" — Broad, open paved plaza with gentle inclines and smooth flagstones; easy to navigate on foot and manageable for strollers. *(Tone: good | Source: TangerLocal)*

---

### 4. Petit Socco (id: tangier-petit-socco)

- **Safety:** Tag "Crowded Alleys" — High-density pedestrian square with constant foot traffic; maintain standard situational awareness of personal items during evening hours. *(Tone: caution | Source: TangerLocal / Lonely Planet)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Faux Guides" — Persistent touts frequently offer directions, souvenir shops, or unwanted tours; ignore unsolicited guiding and navigate independently. *(Tone: caution | Source: Lonely Planet / Departly)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Budget Cafés" — Public square access is free; iconic establishments like Café Central and Café Tingis post low, itemized drink prices (~10–15 MAD). *(Tone: good | Source: TangerLocal)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Literary Heritage" — Tangier's historic bohemian epicenter, famously frequented throughout the 20th century by international writers, beatniks, and local artists. *(Tone: good | Source: Lonely Planet / Barceló)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Cobbled Paths" — Tightly packed medina flagstones with uneven transitions and surrounding step alleys; difficult for wheelchair users. *(Tone: caution | Source: Departly)*

---

### 5. St. Andrew's Anglican Church (id: tangier-st-andrew)

- **Safety:** Tag "Gated Sanctuary" — Enclosed churchyard and garden protected by locked gates outside visiting hours and looked after by an on-site caretaker. *(Tone: good | Source: FOSAT / High Atlas Foundation)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Reputable Caretaker" — Managed respectfully by a dedicated local guardian (Yassine) who provides authentic history without high-pressure sales tactics. *(Tone: good | Source: High Atlas Foundation / FOSAT)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Voluntary Donation" — Admission is technically free; leaving a modest customary donation (~20 MAD per visitor) directly assists site maintenance. *(Tone: good | Source: Wanderlog / AMZBooking)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Moorish-Christian" — Consecrated in 1905 on royal ground gifted by Sultan Hassan I, featuring an authentic Moorish interior with the Lord's Prayer carved in Arabic calligraphy. *(Tone: good | Source: FOSAT / High Atlas Foundation)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Level Grounds" — Flat stone approaches and step-free access into the sanctuary nave, though grass paths through the adjacent historical cemetery can be slightly uneven. *(Tone: good | Source: FOSAT)*

---

### 6. Villa Harris Museum (Musée Villa Harris) (id: tangier-villa-harris)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Secured" — Gated national museum property in Malabata surrounded by fenced municipal botanical gardens with security guards. *(Tone: good | Source: FNM / Activityz Maroc)*
- **Scams:** Tag "State-Operated" — Official cultural museum run by the Fondation Nationale des Musées with a fixed ticketing desk; zero touts. *(Tone: good | Source: FNM)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Fixed Admission" — Standard FNM ticket pricing (20 MAD standard adult admission; free on Fridays for citizens and residents); adjacent gardens are free. *(Tone: good | Source: FNM / SAM.ma)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Modern Masterpieces" — Restored 19th-century residence of journalist Walter Burton Harris, presenting over two centuries of genuine modern and contemporary Moroccan art. *(Tone: good | Source: FNM / Accromaroc)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Park Accessible" — Flat, paved garden promenades and ground-floor gallery ramp access, making it very accessible for strollers and mobility aids. *(Tone: good | Source: Activityz Maroc)*

---

### 7. Dar Niaba Cultural Museum (id: tangier-dar-niaba)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Maintained" — Fully renovated administrative heritage site in the central medina with professional reception and museum attendants. *(Tone: good | Source: FNM / Culturama)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Regulated Entry" — Managed under the umbrella of national cultural institutions; tickets are issued formally with no street-tout involvement. *(Tone: good | Source: FNM / Le360)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Fixed Admission" — Standardized admission fee (~20–30 MAD) displayed at the admissions counter with no surprise add-ons. *(Tone: good | Source: Le360 / Culturama)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Diplomatic Relic" — Tangier's earliest administrative bureau, gifted in 1816 by Sultan Moulay Slimane, housing authentic diplomatic archives and works by historic traveling painters. *(Tone: good | Source: FNM / Le360)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Restored Interior" — Smooth interior flooring across exhibit spaces, but accessing the venue via Rue des Mouahidines involves medina inclines and stone steps. *(Tone: caution | Source: Le360 / Culturama)*

---

### 8. Moshe Nahon Synagogue (id: tangier-moshe-nahon)

- **Safety:** Tag "Quiet Quarter" — Located on a tranquil cul-de-sac in the historic Jewish quarter (Beni Idir); secure gated entry supervised by an on-site guardian. *(Tone: good | Source: Citanger / Wanderlog)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Gatekeeper Access" — Visitors ring the bell for the resident guardian to open; no commercial tour cartels, but a customary tip is expected upon exit. *(Tone: good | Source: Wanderlog / Bazar Travels)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Customary Tip" — No formal ticket counter; visitors customarily leave a small donation or caretaker gratuity (~20 MAD per person). *(Tone: good | Source: Wanderlog / Bazar Travels)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Sephardic Splendor" — Authentic 1878 Andalusian-style synagogue commissioned by scholar Moïse Nahon, adorned with carved stucco, fifty hanging silver lamps, and Hebrew inscriptions. *(Tone: good | Source: Citanger / Morocco Travel Blog)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Narrow Alleys" — Reached via winding medina side-alleys with cobblestones and threshold steps, restricting full wheelchair accessibility. *(Tone: caution | Source: Wanderlog)*

---

### 9. Mendoubia Gardens (Parc de la Mendoubia) (id: tangier-mendoubia-gardens)

- **Safety:** Tag "Daytime Patrolled" — Enclosed city park with staffed municipal gates; pleasant and safe during daylight hours, though unlit corners should be avoided at night. *(Tone: good | Source: Activityz Maroc / Vamoslocal)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Scam-Free" — Pure municipal public park with zero vendors, touts, or unauthorized ticket checkers inside the gated perimeter. *(Tone: good | Source: Activityz Maroc)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Completely Free" — Free admission for all visitors daily from 08:30 to 18:00 without tickets or booking requirements. *(Tone: good | Source: Activityz Maroc / MaJourneys)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Historic Haven" — Heritage municipal park home to an 800-year-old banyan tree, 17th-century bronze cannons, and the monument to Mohammed V's 1947 royal address. *(Tone: good | Source: MaJourneys / Hertz Morocco)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Gently Sloping" — Paved paths and shaded benches across slight inclines, with a few low stone stairways linking garden terraces. *(Tone: good | Source: MaJourneys / Vamoslocal)*

---

### 10. Beit Hahayim (Old Jewish Cemetery of Tangier) (id: tangier-jewish-cemetery)

- **Safety:** Tag "Walled Compound" — Enclosed by perimeter walls on Rue du Portugal; daytime visits are safe, but remote cliffside corners require daylight. *(Tone: good | Source: Archnet / Diarna)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Keyholder Entry" — Knock at the gate for the municipal/community caretaker to unlock access; disregard street touts loitering on adjacent stairs. *(Tone: good | Source: International Jewish Cemetery Project / Wanderlog)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Voluntary Tip" — Publicly owned site with no formal entrance fee; visitors customarily leave a voluntary tip (~10–20 MAD) for the gatekeeper. *(Tone: good | Source: Wanderlog / Archnet)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Centuries-Old" — Historic hillside cemetery holding over 1,000 Sephardic graves dating back centuries, inscribed in Hebrew, Spanish, and Portuguese. *(Tone: good | Source: Archnet / Diarna)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Steep Slope" — Sloping hillside with uneven stone steps, cracked pathways, and overgrown grass; inaccessible for wheelchairs and strollers. *(Tone: caution | Source: International Jewish Cemetery Project)*

---

### 11. Tomb of Ibn Battouta (id: tangier-ibn-battouta-tomb)

- **Safety:** Tag "Enclosed Shrine" — Small sanctified mausoleum located within a calm, residential medina alleyway; quiet and safe during daytime. *(Tone: good | Source: Atlas Obscura / Lonely Planet)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Discretionary Gratuity" — Often kept locked; a local keyholder opens the sanctuary for curious travelers without aggressive touting. *(Tone: good | Source: Atlas Obscura / Lebontrajet)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Small Donation" — No ticket counter; visitors customarily leave a small tip (~10–20 MAD) for the caretaker who opens and maintains the shrine. *(Tone: good | Source: Lebontrajet)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Humble Shrine" — Authentic local zawiya marking the burial site of Tangier's legendary 14th-century world explorer, preserved in simple reverence. *(Tone: good | Source: Atlas Obscura / Lonely Planet)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Steep Medina Alleys" — Reached via uphill climbs along winding medina cobblestones and steps; interior space is very small with a narrow raised entrance. *(Tone: caution | Source: Lonely Planet)*

---

### 12. Darna Association Café & Restaurant (id: tangier-darna-association)

- **Safety:** Tag "Secure Courtyard" — Welcoming community center and women's empowerment NGO with a peaceful, enclosed zellige courtyard and attentive staff. *(Tone: good | Source: Association Darna / Petit Futé)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Licensed Non-Profit" — Formally recognized public-utility NGO; dining revenues directly fund vocational training and housing for disadvantaged women and children. *(Tone: good | Source: Association Darna / Wanderlog)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Fixed Menu" — Transparent blackboard set menu (~70 MAD for starter and main dish; signature lemon tart ~20 MAD); no surprise bills. *(Tone: good | Source: Restaurant Guru / Wanderlog)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Home-Cooked" — Genuine traditional Moroccan home cooking prepared by local women trainees, famous for authentic Friday couscous and tagines. *(Tone: good | Source: Petit Futé / Wanderlog)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Step-Free Access" — Ground-floor courtyard dining situated right off the Grand Socco perimeter, offering easy access with minimal steps. *(Tone: good | Source: Restaurant Guru)*

### 🏜️ Nature & Adventure (7)
### 13. Caves of Hercules (id: tangier-caves-hercules)

- **Safety:** Tag "Slippery Walkways" — Interior handrails and lighting are present, but stone stairways and cavern floors stay wet and slick from ocean humidity and sea spray. *(Tone: caution | Source: Khadamat Culture / MaJourneys)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Aggressive Touts" — Persistent faux guides, souvenir vendors, and camel/monkey handlers crowd the exterior parking lot; bypass them and head straight to the gate. *(Tone: caution | Source: MaJourneys / Blondie in Morocco)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Fixed Admission" — Official Ministry of Culture ticket office with set entry fees (80 MAD for non-resident adults, 30 MAD for Moroccan residents). *(Tone: good | Source: Khadamat Culture)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Millstone Heritage" — Authentic natural limestone sea cavern expanded by centuries of Berber millstone quarrying, famously framing the Atlantic in an Africa-shaped cutout. *(Tone: good | Source: Khadamat Culture / MaJourneys)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Steep Stairs" — Accessing the main viewing chamber requires descending and ascending multiple flights of slick stone stairs; completely inaccessible for wheelchairs. *(Tone: caution | Source: MaJourneys)*

---

### 14. Cap Spartel Lighthouse (id: tangier-cap-spartel)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Guarded" — Enclosed maritime domain featuring perimeter security, staff oversight, and solid cliffside railings overlooking the Atlantic-Mediterranean junction. *(Tone: good | Source: Phare Cap Spartel Official / Accromaroc)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Regulated Facility" — Officially operated heritage compound with standard entry counters; negotiate return grand taxi fares before leaving Tangier to avoid transport markups. *(Tone: good | Source: Blondie in Morocco)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Clear Pricing" — The outer coastal promontory is free; admission to the maritime museum, botanical gardens, and lighthouse climb costs ~25–50 MAD. *(Tone: good | Source: Accromaroc / Phare Cap Spartel Official)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Maritime Heritage" — Morocco's oldest active lighthouse, constructed in 1864 under Sultan Mohammed IV, retaining its historic architecture and original Fresnel lens displays. *(Tone: good | Source: Phare Cap Spartel Official)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Spiral Climb" — Botanical gardens and museum rooms have flat, paved walkways, but ascending the 25-meter tower requires climbing a steep, narrow spiral staircase. *(Tone: caution | Source: Phare Cap Spartel Official)*

---

### 15. Café Hafa (id: tangier-cafe-hafa)

- **Safety:** Tag "Low Ledges" — Cascading open-air terraces built into the cliff face with low stone retaining walls and steep drops; extra care is needed on steps after sunset. *(Tone: caution | Source: Feel Morocco / Wanderlog)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Verbal Billing" — Waiters communicate orders and bills verbally without printed menus; confirm drink prices upon ordering and carry exact small cash to prevent change discrepancies. *(Tone: caution | Source: Wanderlog / minube)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Budget Cash" — No admission charge; classic Moroccan mint tea (~10–15 MAD) and seasonal fava bean bissara (~12 MAD) are paid strictly in cash. *(Tone: good | Source: Où Sortir / Wanderlog)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Bohemian Icon" — Operating continuously since 1921, this unpretentious cultural landmark famously drew Paul Bowles, William S. Burroughs, and generations of local tea drinkers. *(Tone: good | Source: Hertz Morocco / Où Sortir)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Stepped Terraces" — Built entirely down a steep hillside across descending stone terraces connected solely by flights of stairs; completely inaccessible for wheelchairs or strollers. *(Tone: caution | Source: Feel Morocco / Wanderlog)*

### 🌿 Wellness & Living Arts (1)
### 20. Cinémathèque de Tanger (Cinema Rif) (id: tangier-cinematheque)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Secured" — Modernized public theater and cultural center with dedicated venue staff, marked fire exits, and an orderly atmosphere on the Grand Socco. *(Tone: good | Source: Culturama / Cinémathèque de Tanger Official)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Non-Profit Run" — Formally operated by a registered non-profit cultural association; official counter ticketing for all screenings with zero touting or hustling. *(Tone: good | Source: Culturama / NAAS Network)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Clear Pricing" — Free entry to the lobby and terrace café; film screenings cost a fixed, modest rate (~25–40 MAD) and café menus display clear prices. *(Tone: good | Source: Bazar Travels / Morocco Museum)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Arthouse Pioneer" — North Africa's first independent arthouse cinema, housed in the restored 1938 Art Deco Cinéma Rif and actively serving Tangier's local artist and student communities. *(Tone: good | Source: Culturama / Wikipedia)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Easily Accessible" — The plaza terrace café, box office, and ground-floor main auditorium have step-free and low-threshold access directly from Place du 9 Avril 1947. *(Tone: good | Source: Morocco Museum)*

## Taroudant & Tafraoute — 5 listings
> Files: `src/listings/things/taroudant_tafraoute/` — 1 heritage · 4 nature

### 🏛️ Heritage & Culture (1)
### 1. Taroudant Red Mud Ramparts & Bab El Kasbah (id: taroudant-historic-ramparts)

- **Safety:** Tag "Traffic Caution" — The perimeter road and narrow gate arches see steady flows of mopeds, bicycles, and horse carriages; pedestrian vigilance is needed when walking beside the walls. *(Tone: caution | Source: SoussStays / SeniorTravelWithUs)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Negotiate Calèche" — Unofficial guides linger near the main gates and horse-carriage (*calèche*) drivers may quote inflated fares; always negotiate and confirm the full loop price before stepping aboard. *(Tone: caution | Source: Visit Agadir / SoussStays)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Access" — Strolling the historic walls and entering Bab El Kasbah is completely free; hiring a traditional calèche for the full 7.5 km perimeter loop costs a modest ~100–150 MAD per carriage. *(Tone: good | Source: Explore Agadir Souss Massa / SoussStays)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Saadian Adobe" — Exceptional 16th-century Saadian rammed-earth (*pisé*) ramparts enclosing a living, uncommercialized market town where residents go about everyday life. *(Tone: good | Source: CTV News / Explore Agadir Souss Massa)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Flat Circuit" — Ground-level terrain along the walls is completely flat and step-free, though walking the entire 7.5 to 8 km circuit in direct sun requires sun protection and stamina. *(Tone: good | Source: SeniorTravelWithUs / TravelLocal)*

### 🏜️ Nature & Adventure (4)

### 2. Les Peintures Bleues (Blue Painted Rocks of Tafraoute) (id: tafraoute-painted-rocks)

- **Safety:** Tag "Exposed Desert" — Remote open-air desert site with intense sun exposure, rocky dirt tracks, and no on-site amenities; carry plenty of drinking water and drive slowly on unpaved sections. *(Tone: caution | Source: Explore Agadir Souss Massa / MoroccoTravelBase)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Ungated Site" — An open natural landscape with no admission gates, commercial touts, or unauthorized guides; completely peaceful and self-guided. *(Tone: good | Source: MapTrotting / MoroccoTravelBase)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Completely Free" — Free public access with zero entrance fees, parking charges, or advance reservations required to explore the boulder field. *(Tone: good | Source: The Painted Rocks of Tafraoute / MapTrotting)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Monumental Art" — Renowned 1984 environmental land-art installation executed by Belgian artist Jean Vérame using eco-friendly pigment, set amid dramatic Anti-Atlas granite peaks. *(Tone: good | Source: Explore Agadir Souss Massa / UNESCO)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Rocky Scramble" — Flat dirt tracks connect the main clusters, but climbing and maneuvering among the monumental boulders involves uneven gravel and loose rock under the sun. *(Tone: caution | Source: MapTrotting / MTB Morocco)*

---

### 3. Tiout Oasis & Saadian Kasbah (id: tiout-oasis-kasbah)

- **Safety:** Tag "Crumbling Ruins" — Historic kasbah ramparts contain unrestored, crumbling stone sections and low ledges; palm grove trails involve stepping across earthen irrigation ditches (*seguias*). *(Tone: caution | Source: Morocco Shiny Days / Immersi Travel)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Negotiate Guides" — Local donkey handlers and informal guides greet visitors at the entrance; firmly agree on donkey rental (~50–100 MAD) or guiding rates before beginning the tour. *(Tone: caution | Source: TakeYourBackpack / Immersi Travel)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Admission" — Walking into the oasis and visiting the kasbah promontory is completely free; optional donkey rides and tagines at the kasbah terrace restaurant carry modest, payable local rates. *(Tone: good | Source: Immersi Travel / TakeYourBackpack)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Living Oasis" — An authentic working Amazigh agricultural oasis of thousands of date palms fed by centuries-old canal systems, topped by an iconic 16th-century Saadian fortress. *(Tone: good | Source: Explore Agadir Souss Massa / VisitRuralMorocco)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Moderate Incline" — Climbing up to the kasbah requires an uphill walk along a stony slope, while the shady palm grove paths are predominantly level with minor ditch crossings. *(Tone: caution | Source: Naturally Morocco / Morocco Shiny Days)*

## Tetouan & Martil — 20 listings
> Files: `src/listings/things/tetouan_martil/` — 10 heritage · 10 nature

### 🏛️ Heritage & Culture (10)
### 1. Tetouan Medina (id: tetouan-martil-tetouan-medina)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Patrolled" — Active tourist police posts and low street crime; very safe to explore in daylight, though unlit peripheral alleys warrant caution after dark. *(Tone: good | Source: UNESCO / Lonely Planet)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Low-Pressure" — Remarkably free from the aggressive touting and persistent faux guides common in larger medinas; shopkeepers are courteous and uninsistent. *(Tone: good | Source: Rough Guides / Lonely Planet)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Access" — Exploring the walled medina is completely free; artisanal goods, market produce, and street snacks carry authentic local Dirham prices. *(Tone: good | Source: Serenity Morocco Tours)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "UNESCO Preserved" — A UNESCO World Heritage site and an intact 15th-century Andalusian-Moroccan enclave of living artisan guilds, traditional tanneries, and residential life. *(Tone: good | Source: UNESCO)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Cobbled Inclines" — Sloping hillside alleys built into Jbel Dersa with cobblestones, drainage gullies, and stone steps, restricting wheelchair and stroller access. *(Tone: caution | Source: Accessible Travel / Lonely Planet)*

---

### 2. Royal Palace & Hassan II Square (id: tetouan-martil-royal-palace-and-hassan-ii-square)

- **Safety:** Tag "Strictly Guarded" — Highly secure perimeter under 24/7 protection by the Moroccan Royal Guard and national police; very safe for public walks. *(Tone: good | Source: Lonely Planet / Petit Futé)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Scam-Free" — Touts and unlicensed guides are strictly banned from loitering in the square; photography of the palace gates or guards is strictly prohibited. *(Tone: good | Source: Lonely Planet)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Access" — Strolling the public esplanade is entirely free; the Royal Palace itself is an active sovereign residence and strictly closed to the public. *(Tone: good | Source: Lonely Planet / Petit Futé)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Hispano-Moorish" — Authentic 17th-century Alaouite royal palace restored in the 1940s, framed by modernist wrought-iron street lamps designed by Spanish painter Mariano Bertuchi. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Tetouan / Lonely Planet)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Level Paved" — Wide, completely flat flagstone esplanade with zero elevation change or steps, providing effortless mobility for wheelchairs and strollers. *(Tone: good | Source: Lonely Planet)*

---

### 3. Archaeological Museum of Tetouan (id: tetouan-martil-archaeological-museum-of-tetouan)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Monitored" — Enclosed institutional museum facility with attentive staff, indoor security, and sheltered courtyard walkways. *(Tone: good | Source: Min Culture Maroc / FNM)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Official Counter" — Managed under national cultural heritage guidelines with fixed tickets issued at the reception desk; no unauthorized guides. *(Tone: good | Source: MaJourneys / FNM)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Fixed Admission" — Standardized admission fee (30 MAD for standard adult entry, 15 MAD reduced) clearly posted at the ticket counter. *(Tone: good | Source: MaJourneys / Activityz Maroc)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Ancient Artifacts" — Houses genuine prehistoric, Punic, and Roman antiquities excavated from northern Moroccan sites, including celebrated mosaics from Lixus and Tamuda. *(Tone: good | Source: UNESCO / MaJourneys)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Mostly Flat" — Ground-floor mosaic courtyard and galleries are step-free and accessible; only the upper-floor numismatic collection requires climbing stairs. *(Tone: good | Source: MaJourneys)*

---

### 4. Ethnographic Museum (id: tetouan-martil-ethnographic-museum)

- **Safety:** Tag "Sturdy Ramparts" — Housed within the historic Sultan Moulay Abderrahmane fortress (Bab el-Okla bastion) with municipal staff and well-maintained exhibits. *(Tone: good | Source: Aujourd'hui le Maroc / Petit Futé)*
- **Scams:** Tag "State-Run" — Official public museum with gate ticketing; completely free of commercial pressure, souvenir pushing, or faux guides. *(Tone: good | Source: Petit Futé / Vanupied)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Fixed Admission" — Government-regulated entry fee (~20–50 MAD) with official receipts, supporting cultural preservation. *(Tone: good | Source: Vanupied / Petit Futé)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Living Traditions" — Showcases genuine Tetouani cultural heritage, including antique wedding dresses (*keswa ikbira*), Jebala folk garments, painted woodwork, and traditional musical instruments. *(Tone: good | Source: Aujourd'hui le Maroc / Petit Futé)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Fortress Steps" — Historic 19th-century bastion layout with narrow stone stairways and elevated terrace thresholds, limiting accessibility for mobility-impaired visitors. *(Tone: caution | Source: Petit Futé / Michelin)*

---

### 5. Dar Sanaa Craft School (id: tetouan-martil-dar-sanaa-craft-school)

- **Safety:** Tag "Institutional Setting" — Formal vocational school operated under the Ministry of Culture; quiet, supervised workshop studios and tranquil interior courtyards. *(Tone: good | Source: Culturama / MaJourneys)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Non-Commercial" — Genuine artisan training academy rather than a tourist souvenir shop; students train in classes without aggressive sales pitches. *(Tone: good | Source: MaJourneys / Sahara Discovery Tours)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Regulated Ticket" — Official Ministry of Culture admission (60 MAD for foreign adults, 30 MAD for residents) purchased at the gate; free on certain national holidays. *(Tone: good | Source: MaJourneys / Morocco Museum)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Living Apprenticeship" — Founded in 1919 by Mariano Bertuchi, keeping alive centuries-old master traditions of zellige tile chiseling, plaster carving, leatherwork, and embroidery. *(Tone: good | Source: UNESCO / MaJourneys)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Courtyard Steps" — Neo-Moorish architectural layout with level ground-floor courtyard access, though step thresholds into individual workshops can hinder wheelchairs. *(Tone: caution | Source: Morocco Museum)*

---

### 6. Ensanche Spanish Quarter Walk (id: tetouan-martil-ensanche-spanish-quarter-walk)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Lit" — Broad, central European-style boulevards with continuous police presence, pedestrian walkways, and lively foot traffic well into the night. *(Tone: good | Source: Lonely Planet / Le Desk)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Scam-Free" — Regular commercial downtown district populated by local businesses, bookshops, and banks; completely free of tourist hustling. *(Tone: good | Source: Rough Guides)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Promenade" — Public city walking route with no access fees; sidewalk cafés (like Café Colón on Place Moulay El Mehdi) feature printed, inexpensive menus. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Tetouan)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Colonial Architecture" — Intact early 20th-century Spanish Protectorate urbanism blending Andalusian Revival, Art Deco, and Neo-Moorish facades along Avenue Mohammed V. *(Tone: good | Source: Le Desk / UNESCO)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Paved Sidewalks" — Wide, flat paved sidewalks with gentle street grades and curb ramps, making it fully accessible for strollers and wheelchair users. *(Tone: good | Source: Lonely Planet)*

---

### 7. Place Feddan (id: tetouan-martil-place-feddan)

- **Safety:** Tag "Pedestrian Zone" — Expansive, completely vehicle-free public esplanade with bright evening floodlighting and municipal attendants; exceptionally safe for families. *(Tone: good | Source: Accromaroc / Le Desk)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Scam-Free" — Popular community promenade for Tetouani families; no organized touting, faux guides, or aggressive vendors on the square. *(Tone: good | Source: Accromaroc)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Completely Free" — Free public square open 24/7; adjacent multi-level underground parking charges modest standard municipal hourly rates. *(Tone: good | Source: Accromaroc / Le Desk)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Andalusian Esplanade" — Reconstructed two-hectare Andalusian-style open plaza featuring traditional white arcades, zellige pavilions, and sweeping panoramic views of the medina hillside. *(Tone: good | Source: Le Desk / Accromaroc)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Step-Free" — Perfectly flat stone and tile paving with wide ramps connecting to surrounding boulevards, offering seamless stroller and wheelchair access. *(Tone: good | Source: Accromaroc)*

---

### 8. Bab el-Okla Gate Walk (id: tetouan-martil-bab-el-okla-gate-walk)

- **Safety:** Tag "Lively Thoroughfare" — Active medina gateway with constant foot traffic and daylight activity; take care when handcarts pass through the arch. *(Tone: good | Source: Petit Futé / Virtual Trip)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Self-Guided" — Free public street gate; ignore self-appointed street guides offering unprompted tours into the nearby leather tanning quarter. *(Tone: good | Source: Lonely Planet)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Access" — Walking through the monumental gate and viewing the historic public fountain (Saqiya Bab el-Okla) is completely free of charge. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Tetouan)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Historic Gateway" — Authentic 16th-century fortified portal into the eastern medina, featuring original stone masonry and an ornate 18th-century polychrome zellige fountain. *(Tone: good | Source: UNESCO / Min Culture Maroc)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Gentle Incline" — Paved stone entrance with a slight gradient transitioning into the medina; cobblestones can be slick in rainy weather. *(Tone: good | Source: Petit Futé)*

---

### 9. Tamuda Ruins (id: tetouan-martil-tamuda-ruins)

- **Safety:** Tag "Exposed Ruins" — Fenced open-air archaeological park with no natural shade; uneven ancient masonry and scrub grass require careful footing and sun protection. *(Tone: caution | Source: Khadamat Culture / Morocco Museum)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Guardian-Tended" — Peaceful archaeological station tended by official Ministry of Culture guardians; zero souvenir hawkers or tourist touts. *(Tone: good | Source: Khadamat Culture / JetlyGo)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Fixed Admission" — Official Ministry of Culture ticket counter (80 MAD for foreign adults, 30 MAD for Moroccan residents); free on national holidays. *(Tone: good | Source: Khadamat Culture)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Ancient Roman" — Authentic 3rd-century BC Mauretanian settlement and 1st-century AD Roman military castellum along the Martil River, ancestor of modern Tetouan. *(Tone: good | Source: Khadamat Culture / UNESCO)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Rough Terrain" — Unpaved dirt footpaths, loose stones, and excavated trench banks make navigating the ruins impossible for wheelchairs and difficult for strollers. *(Tone: caution | Source: Morocco Museum)*

---

### 10. Tetouan Center of Modern Art (id: tetouan-martil-tetouan-center-of-modern-art)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Secured" — Enclosed institutional museum facility housed in a restored heritage station with on-site attendants and secure gallery rooms. *(Tone: good | Source: e-taqafa / Vanupied)*
- **Scams:** Tag "State-Operated" — Jointly operated under cultural ministry oversight; official entrance process with zero commercial touts or solicitations. *(Tone: good | Source: e-taqafa / CityWays)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Admission" — Public cultural museum offering free admission (or a nominal token fee during specific guest exhibitions). *(Tone: good | Source: Vanupied / Culturama)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Neo-Moorish Depot" — Restored 1918 Spanish railway terminal celebrating four generations of the renowned National Institute of Fine Arts (INBA) of Tetouan. *(Tone: good | Source: e-taqafa / Le Desk)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Step-Free" — Ground-level former railway concourse featuring wide entrance doorways, flat thresholds, and smooth floors, fully accessible for wheelchairs. *(Tone: good | Source: e-taqafa / Vanupied)*

### 🏜️ Nature & Adventure (10)
### 11. Ras El Ma Spring (id: tetouan-martil-ras-el-ma-spring)

- **Safety:** Tag "Unfenced Waters" — Open natural spring area with unpaved irrigation banks and running water channels; no lifeguards on-site, requiring attention with young children. *(Tone: caution | Source: Moroccan Mate / Morocco Geo)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Ungated Site" — A natural public leisure spot with no gatekeepers, ticket sellers, or tout syndicates; completely peaceful and self-guided. *(Tone: good | Source: Moroccan Mate)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Completely Free" — Free public natural area with zero entrance charges, parking permits, or commercial access fees. *(Tone: good | Source: Moroccan Mate)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Rural Waterways" — A genuine edge-of-city natural spring feeding the Semssa valley's traditional irrigation network, frequented by local families for weekend picnics. *(Tone: good | Source: Morocco Geo / Moroccan Mate)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Rustic Paths" — Dirt tracks, unpaved grassy clearings, and earthen canal borders demand flat footwear; unsuitable for wheelchairs or strollers. *(Tone: caution | Source: Moroccan Mate)*

---

### 12. Martil Corniche (id: tetouan-martil-martil-corniche)

- **Safety:** Tag "Well-Patrolled" — Broad, brightly illuminated seaside pedestrian boulevard with steady municipal and police oversight; very safe for evening family strolls. *(Tone: good | Source: Visit Tetouan / TakeYourBackpack)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Low-Pressure" — Relaxed beachfront strip free from aggressive touting or faux guides; confirm rates with casual snack and pedal-cart vendors before purchasing. *(Tone: good | Source: TakeYourBackpack / Moroccan Mate)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Promenade" — Walking the promenade is completely free; lined cafés, ice cream shops, and fish eateries display clear, standardized menus. *(Tone: good | Source: Moroccan Mate / TakeYourBackpack)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Domestic Riviera" — An authentic Moroccan seaside promenade popular with domestic holidaymakers, university students, and local families enjoying evening *paseos*. *(Tone: good | Source: TakeYourBackpack / Moroccan Mate)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Step-Free" — Wide, completely flat tiled promenade with continuous curbside ramps and zero steps, fully accessible for wheelchairs and strollers. *(Tone: good | Source: Moroccan Mate / Farid Explore)*

---

### 13. Martil Beach (id: tetouan-martil-martil-beach)

- **Safety:** Tag "Seasonally Lifeguarded" — Mediterranean waters are generally calm and shallow, with Protection Civile lifeguards patrolling designated family swim zones in summer. *(Tone: good | Source: Pack Lightly / Agoda)*
- **Scams:** Tag "Unregulated Rentals" — Beachfront umbrella and plastic lounger operators lack posted price tags; always confirm the daily rental cost before sitting down. *(Tone: caution | Source: TakeYourBackpack / Agoda)*
- **Pricing:** Tag "Free Access" — The entire sandy public beach has free open entry; optional umbrella and sunbed rentals cost modest, negotiable rates (~30–50 MAD). *(Tone: good | Source: TakeYourBackpack / Visit Tetouan)*
- **Authenticity:** Tag "Domestic Resort" — A bustling community holiday beach favored by Moroccan vacationers and northern families rather than an artificial tourist enclave. *(Tone: good | Source: Pack Lightly / Moroccan Mate)*
- **Difficulty:** Tag "Soft Sand" — Promenade access ramps lead to the sand, but walking across the wide, deep dry sand down to the water requires moderate physical effort. *(Tone: caution | Source: TakeYourBackpack / Moroccan Mate)*
